Um, it's more just... frustrating, I guess? Like I mentioned, I'm mostly just worried about how it looks to my boss. Um, these problems with my attention and trying to stay organized... they've been noticeably harder for me to manage ever since I started an evening certificate program. That was about 15 months ago.\n\nDoctor: Are these memory and organizational issues affecting your work or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still getting by for the most part. But I've missed small work follow-ups and, um, a few school deadlines often enough that it's really starting to worry me. I want to move into an Office Manager role eventually, and I'm just afraid that forgetting these little things is going to affect my reputation at work and hurt my chances of getting that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed by these memory and organizational issues?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel anxious or completely overwhelmed, no. Um, it's more just... frustrating, I guess? Like I mentioned, I'm mostly just worried about how it looks to my boss. But I'm not, you know, having panic attacks or feeling like I can't handle my life. It's just annoying that I keep dropping the ball on these simple things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel depressed or anything like that. Just... like I said, mostly just frustrated with myself when I mess up at work or school. But my mood overall is fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble learning new things for your certificate program?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, the actual material makes sense to me. I don't have a hard time understanding or learning the new concepts for the classes. It's really just... remembering to turn the assignments in on time, like I was saying before. But grasping the information itself hasn't been an issue at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not exactly. I don't think I have a problem paying attention when I'm actually doing something. Like, if I'm working on a task, I can focus on it just fine. It's more about organizing things and remembering the details or deadlines, rather than not being able to focus on the work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel mentally foggy or like your thinking is cloudy?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get that. My thinking feels pretty clear most of the time. Like I was saying, it's just keeping track of all the different pieces of information that gets messy for me, but my head itself doesn't feel foggy or cloudy.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when speaking or writing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I write a lot of emails and talk on the phone all day for my job, and I don't usually get stuck trying to find the right words. Communication is actually pretty easy for me, so I wouldn't say I struggle with that at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But I've missed small work follow-ups and, um, a few school deadlines often enough that it's really starting to worry me. I want to move into an Office Manager role eventually, and I'm just afraid that forgetting these little things is going to affect my reputation at work and hurt my chances of getting that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed by these memory and organizational issues?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel anxious or completely overwhelmed, no. Um, it's more just... frustrating, I guess? Like I mentioned, I'm mostly just worried about how it looks to my boss. But I'm not, you know, having panic attacks or feeling like I can't handle my life. It's just annoying that I keep dropping the ball on these simple things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel depressed or anything like that. Just... like I said, mostly just frustrated with myself when I mess up at work or school. But my mood overall is fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble learning new things for your certificate program?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, the actual material makes sense to me. I don't have a hard time understanding or learning the new concepts for the classes. It's really just... remembering to turn the assignments in on time, like I was saying before. But grasping the information itself hasn't been an issue at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not exactly. I don't think I have a problem paying attention when I'm actually doing something. Like, if I'm working on a task, I can focus on it just fine. It's more about organizing things and remembering the details or deadlines, rather than not being able to focus on the work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel mentally foggy or like your thinking is cloudy?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get that. My thinking feels pretty clear most of the time. Like I was saying, it's just keeping track of all the different pieces of information that gets messy for me, but my head itself doesn't feel foggy or cloudy.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when speaking or writing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I write a lot of emails and talk on the phone all day for my job, and I don't usually get stuck trying to find the right words. But since I started taking evening classes, planning has gotten a lot harder. My desk, my class folder, all my email reminders... they just kind of turn into piles. I end up wasting a lot of time just trying to figure out what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My memory for things that happened in the past is actually pretty good. I don't have any trouble recalling events or conversations from a while ago. It's really just the current, day-to-day stuff that gets jumbled up for me, but my long-term memory is fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble making decisions or prioritizing tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I don't really struggle with that part.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble recalling things that you were supposed to do, but forgot to do them?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. That is what finally made me ask for help. I just keep forgetting little things that I normally handle without any issue. Like, um, returning phone calls, or turning in a registration form, or even just remembering to stop for an errand on my way home. I don't know for sure. It's just everyday stuff, like returning phone calls at work, or turning in a simple registration form. Or even just remembering to stop for a quick errand on the way home. It's just happening a lot lately, and I need to figure out why.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these memory problems constantly over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Actually, it's been going on for a lot longer than just a few weeks. Um, these problems with my attention and trying to stay organized... they've been noticeably harder for me to manage ever since I started an evening certificate program. That was about 15 months ago.\n\nDoctor: Are these memory and organizational issues affecting your work or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still getting by for the most part. But I've missed small work follow-ups and, um, a few school deadlines often enough that it's really starting to worry me. I want to move into an Office Manager role eventually, and I'm just afraid that forgetting these little things is going to affect my reputation at work and hurt my chances of getting that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed by these memory and organizational issues?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel anxious or completely overwhelmed, no. Um, it's more just... frustrating, I guess? Like I mentioned, I'm mostly just worried about how it looks to my boss. But I'm not, you know, having panic attacks or feeling like I can't handle my life. It's just annoying that I keep dropping the ball on these simple things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel depressed or anything like that. Just... like I said, mostly just frustrated with myself when I mess up at work or school. But my mood overall is fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble learning new things for your certificate program?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, the actual material makes sense to me. I don't have a hard time understanding or learning the new concepts for the classes. It's really just... remembering to turn the assignments in on time, like I was saying before. But grasping the information itself hasn't been an issue at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not exactly. I don't think I have a problem paying attention when I'm actually doing something. Like, if I'm working on a task, I can focus on it just fine. It's more about organizing things and remembering the details or deadlines, rather than not being able to focus on the work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel mentally foggy or like your thinking is cloudy?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get that. My thinking feels pretty clear most of the time. Like I was saying, it's just keeping track of all the different pieces of information that gets messy for me, but my head itself doesn't feel foggy or cloudy.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when speaking or writing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I write a lot of emails and talk on the phone all day for my job, and I don't usually get stuck trying to find the right words. Communication is actually pretty easy for me, so I wouldn't say I struggle with that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble planning out your day or week ahead of time?\n\nPatient: Well, usually I can keep up just fine at work. But since I started taking evening classes, planning has gotten a lot harder. My desk, my class folder, all my email reminders... they just kind of turn into piles. I end up wasting a lot of time just trying to figure out what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My memory for things that happened in the past is actually pretty good. I don't have any trouble recalling events or conversations from a while ago. It's really just the current, day-to-day stuff that gets jumbled up for me, but my long-term memory is fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble making decisions or prioritizing tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. Making decisions isn't really a problem for me. And as for prioritizing... well, as a secretary, figuring out what needs to get done first is basically my whole job. So I'm pretty used to it. I don't really struggle with that part.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble recalling things that you were supposed to do, but forgot to do them?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. That is what finally made me ask for help. I just keep forgetting little things that I normally handle without any issue. Like, um, returning phone calls, or turning in a registration form, or even just remembering to stop for an errand on my way home. It's those kinds of everyday things that just keep slipping my mind lately.\n\nDoctor: When you forget to do something, are you usually doing something else at the same time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that. I mean, at work I'm usually juggling a few different things, so I guess I could be in the middle of something else when a thought slips away, but... I don't know for sure. It just feels like the reminder is suddenly gone, regardless of what I'm physically doing at that exact moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think if someone reminded you about these tasks, you would be able to remember to do them?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure. I mean, maybe? I haven't really had anyone do that for me, so I don't know for a fact. I guess if they reminded me right at the exact moment I needed to do it, it would probably help. But if they told me in the morning and I had to do it later in the afternoon... I don't know if I'd still remember or if it would just slip away again. I just haven't really had that happen to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually remember something right after you learn it or are told to do it, but then forget it later?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I can say it happens like that. I mean, I don't usually notice that I've forgotten something until... well, until I realize I didn't do it. So I'm not really tracking if I knew it right after I was told and then lost it later. It's just... suddenly it's not done, and I realize I completely forgot about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
